I grab out data between these time points to correspond to trials. \n\nii. \n```python\ndef get_trial_starts_ends(trial_start,teleport):\n    trial_start_idx = np.nonzero(trial_start)[0]\n    is_teleport_start = (teleport[1:] > 0) & (teleport[:-1] <= 0)\n    trial_end_idx = np.nonzero(is_teleport_start)[0] + 1 \n    assert len(trial_start_idx) == len(trial_end_idx), f\"Number of trial starts ({len(trial_start_idx)}) does not match number of trial ends ({len(trial_end_idx)})\"\n    return trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx\n...\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n...\nteleport = behavior_data['teleport'].data[:]\ntrial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], teleport)\n```\n\niii. The paper computes its own neural signal rather than using a stored one in `preprocessing.dff(F, Fneu, ..., deconvolve=True)` and stores the result as `sess.timeseries['events']`. How is the `neural` data processed? \n\ni. dF/F is computed from F and Fneu and then deconvolved into \"events\", by a copy of the paper's own `preprocessing.dff`. Per plane: restrict to the lap samples (or lap plus teleport, depending on the session), subtract `0.7 * Fneu`, add each trial's mean neuropil back so the ratio is a true dF/F, take a maximin baseline (Gaussian smoothing with sigma 15 samples, then a 300-sample running minimum followed by a 300-sample running maximum, the Methods' 20 s window), form `(F - baseline)/|baseline|`, smooth with a 2-sample Gaussian, and deconvolve with OASIS at `tau = 0.7` and `frame_rate/n_planes`. Cells from multiple planes are pooled afterwards.\n\nWhether the baseline window may span the teleport is a per-mouse, per-day fact, copied from `teleport_metadata.py`: the laser was blanked between trials except on the days listed there, where imaging continued. It matters -- on m15 day 5, spanning the teleport widens the baseline window by 35% and moves the emitted events by ~9% (median per-cell r = 0.986).\n\nii.\n```python\ndff_curr, events_curr = dff(fluorescence_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            pd.Series(trial_start_idx + 1), pd.Series(trial_end_idx + 1),\n                            f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n                            subtract_baseline=True, neu_coef=0.7, tau=0.7,\n                            frame_rate=frame_rate, n_planes=nplanes, deconvolve=True,\n                            keep_teleports=keep_teleports)\n```\n\niii. This is the processing the Methods describe: \"baseline fluorescence was calculated within each trial independently using a maximin procedure with a 20 s sliding window ... dF/F was then calculated for each cell as the fluorescence minus the baseline, divided by the absolute value of the baseline, then smoothed with a two-sample (~0.129 s) s.d. Gaussian kernel. The activity rate was extracted by deconvolving dF/F with a canonical calcium kernel using the OASIS algorithm as used in Suite2p.\" The parameters are theirs: `neu_coef = 0.7` and `baseline_method = 'maximin'` from `notebooks/make_multi_anim_sess.ipynb`, `tau = 0.7` from the suite2p ops in `notebooks/suite2p_notebooks/example_m12.ipynb`.\n\nThe neural sampling rate is the same for all recordings once the multi-plane sessions are accounted for. Note that the `rate` stored on the NWB series is the scanner rate, so on the two-plane sessions it is 31 Hz and the per-plane rate -- the one that sets both the time bin and the deconvolution kernel -- is `rate/nplanes`.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters, both the paper's. Cells are first restricted to the `iscell` ROIs, which carry suite2p's manual curation. Putative interneurons are then dropped: any cell whose dF/F correlates with the animal's running speed at r > 0.5.\n\nii. \n```python\niscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\nfluorescence.append(fluorescence_curr[:,iscell])\nneuropil.append(neuropil_curr[:,iscell])\n...\nisint = is_putative_interneuron(dff_curr, speed)\nspike_data.append(events_curr[~isint].T)\n```\n\niii. `iscell` is the manual curation the Methods describe. Reward event timestamps are matched to behavior timestamps, and a binary `isreward` array marks timepoints where rewards occurred.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\nreward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n```\n\niii. The `Reward` time series has its own timestamps (not aligned to the behavior sampling rate), so `searchsorted` is used to find the nearest timepoint. An assertion checks that the alignment error is within half a time bin.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. For each trial, check whether any reward event occurred in the *previous* trial's time range. For the first trial, set to 0. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nif trial == 0:\n    input_curr[3,:] = 0\nelse:\n    prev_reward_idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial-1], trial_end_idx[trial-1], dtype=int)\n    isprevreward = np.any(isreward[prev_reward_idx])\n    input_curr[3,:] = int(isprevreward)\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify previous trial outcome as binary (omitted=0, rewarded=1). The code checks if any reward event fell within the previous trial's time boundaries.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n\nii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n```python\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \ninfo['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    pos_trial = position[idx]\n    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n        continue\n    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n```\nThe function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n...\nsigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n```\nIt also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n```python\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. The paper defines reward zones A, B, C at specific position ranges. These same ranges could also be found, with some translation, in the code (required mapping X -> A, etc.). I plotted the position of the mouse when `reward_zone` was positive (note that the value was between 0 and 6 I think) to determine that the Viterbi approach would likely work, and that the start of the rewarded position was the least noisy variable to use. The class fractions were approximately 1/3 after this, so it seemed correct. \n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it.  The continuous distance is then discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n\n```\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\n\niii. This matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The reward zone boundaries are from `reward_zone_dict`, inspired by the reference code `Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the instructions. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The position data and neural data share the same time indices within each trial, so no additional alignment is needed.\n\nii.\n```python\nidx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\nspike_data_curr = spike_data[idx]\nposition_curr = position[idx]\n```\n\niii. Both neural and behavioral data use the same time indexing (verified by timestamp assertions earlier in the code), so indexing by the same `idx` array ensures alignment.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:][:T]\nassert np.allclose(behavior_data['position'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for position do not match trial number timestamps\"\n...\nposition_curr = position[idx]\n```\n\niii. The `position` variable directly records the animal's position in the VR corridor in cm.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. No processing beyond extracting the per-trial slice and discretizing. Discretized into 5 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, 90, 180, 270, 360, inf]`, corresponding to 90 cm wide bins spanning the 450 cm track. Within the converted trials the position ranges from -0.8 to 451.8 cm, so the open first and last bins absorb the few samples that fall just outside the track (185 of 1,307,665). \n\nii.\n```python\nposition_curr = position[idx]\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\n```python\nposition_bins = [-np.inf,90,180,270,360,np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\n\niii. The raw position values are used directly. Each lap starts at 0 cm and the track is 450 cm long (Methods), so five 90 cm bins over 0 to 450 are equal-sized by construction. The survey's wider -50 to 450 range comes from including the inter-trial teleport period, which is not part of any converted trial.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 5 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, 90, 180, 270, 360, inf]`, corresponding to 90 cm wide bins spanning the 450 cm track. \n\nii.\n```python\nposition_bins = [-np.inf,90,180,270,360,np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify \"5 equal-sized bins\" spanning the 450 cm track, which is 90 cm per bin. The first and last edges are left open so that samples marginally outside the track fall in the end bins rather than in a class of their own.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as the neural data within each trial — no additional alignment needed.\n\nii. Same indexing as neural: `position_curr = position[idx]`\n\niii. Verified by timestamp assertions.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `lick` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nlicks = behavior_data['lick'].data[:][:T]\nassert np.allclose(behavior_data['lick'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for lick do not match trial number timestamps\"\n...\nlicks_curr = licks[idx]\n```\n\niii. The `lick` variable records lick events at each timepoint.\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick*?\n\ni. Binarized: any positive lick value is mapped to 1, otherwise 0.\n\nii.\n```python\noutput_curr[3] = (licks_curr > 0).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify binary output (no/yes). The raw lick values can be >1, so thresholding at >0 converts to binary.\n\n## 9-c. How is `output` *Lick* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as the neural data within each trial — no additional alignment needed.\n\nii. Same indexing as neural: `licks_curr = licks[idx]`\n\niii. Verified by timestamp assertions.\n\n## 10-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward zone location* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `reward_zone` behavior time series and `position` behavior time series, as described in answer 7-a. \n\nii. See answer 7-a.\n\niii. See answer 7-a.\n\n## 10-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward zone location*?\n\ni. See answer 7-a. \n\nii. See answer 7-a.\n\niii. See answer 7-a. \n\n## 11-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `Reward` behavior time series. Reward events have their own timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n...\noutput_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. The `Reward` time series records reward delivery events with separate timestamps from the behavior sampling rate.\n\n## 11-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward outcome*?\n\ni. Reward event timestamps are mapped to the nearest behavior timepoint using `searchsorted`. For each trial, the output is 1 if any reward event occurred within the trial, 0 otherwise. The value is constant across all timepoints in the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nerr = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\nassert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n...\noutput_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. The alignment error is checked to be within half a time bin. `np.any` is used because a trial either was rewarded or not — the specific timepoint doesn't matter for the per-trial binary output.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. These are defensive checks found during data exploration. The neural/behavior mismatch occurs in a small number of sessions.\n\n## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are:\n1. **Loading NWB files** (`pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` and reading large arrays) — I/O bound\n2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n3. **Full conversion** — loads every NWB file again and extracts all trial data\n4. **Saving the .pkl file**\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The NWB files are large (contain full neural recordings). Conversion is otherwise quite fast\n\n## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The per-trial loop is the natural structure given variable-length trials. Vectorizing would require padding or masking.\n\n## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n\nii. N/A\n\niii. I don't think these could be run at the same time, as it would require too much RAM. The Viterbi output created during surveying is required to process the data. It was convenient to run the survey before deciding how to handle the conversion of the data. \n\n## 13-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. N/A\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 13-e. How is memory usage optimized?\n\ni. N/A\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\nimport pynwb\nimport os\nimport warnings\nimport numpy as np\nimport matplotlib\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport pickle\nimport argparse\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy.interpolate import interp1d\nfrom scipy import ndimage\nfrom suite2p.extraction import dcnv\n\nDATADIR = 'data'\nSURVEYFILE = 'survey_info.pkl'\n\n_sosa2024_seed = 'x5cidj2hy87s'\nnp.random.seed(sum(ord(c) for c in _sosa2024_seed) % 2**31)\n\ninput_names = ['time_from_trial_start','environment','trial_number','previous_reward_outcome']\noutput_names = ['distance_to_reward_zone','position','speed','lick','reward_location','reward_outcome']\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\nposition_bins = [-np.inf,90,180,270,360,np.inf]\nspeed_bins = [-np.inf, 2, 10, 20, 40, np.inf]\noutput_values = [\n    [\n        '< -50 cm',\n        '-50 to -10 cm',\n        '-10 cm to < 0 cm',\n        '0 cm',\n        '>0 cm to +10 cm',\n        '+10 to +50 cm',\n        '> +50 cm'\n    ],\n    [\n        '0 to 90 cm',\n        '90 to 180 cm',\n        '180 to 270 cm',\n        '270 to 360 cm',\n        '360 to 450 cm',\n    ],\n    [\n        '< 2 cm/s',\n        '2 to 10 cm/s',\n        '10 to 20 cm/s',\n        '20 to 40 cm/s',\n        '> 40 cm/s',\n    ],\n    ['no', 'yes'],\n    ['A', 'B', 'C'],\n    ['no','yes']\n]\n\n# inspired by Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n\n# =================================================================================================\n# The paper's neural signal processing, COPIED from the paper's code repo\n#\n# The NWB \"Deconvolved\" array is NOT the signal the paper analyses. It is suite2p's own \n# deconvolution of raw fluorescence, and not used in the paper analyses. \n# =================================================================================================\n\n# Experiment days on which the laser was NOT blanked between trials, so the dF/F baseline window is\n# allowed to span the teleport. Animals missing from this table (m3, m4 and m7) were\n# blanked on every day, so their baselines are always restricted to the lap.\n# ** copied from src/reward_relative/teleport_metadata.py::teleport_sessions **\n\n# Dictionary of animals and experiment days for which the teleport periods were imaged\n# (as opposed to blanking the laser)\n\nteleport_sessions = {\n    'GCAMP10': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    'GCAMP11': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    'GCAMP12': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    'GCAMP13': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    'GCAMP14': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    'GCAMP15': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n    'GCAMP17': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n    'GCAMP18': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n    'GCAMP19': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n}\n\n\ndef nansmooth(a, sig):\n    \"\"\"\n    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n    without propagating nans\n    :param a:\n    :param sig:\n    :return:\n\n    ** copied from TwoPUtils/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = .001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"\n    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n    without propagating nans\n    :param a:\n    :param sig:\n    :return:\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n\n    out = a_nanless / one\n    if return_nans:\n        out[nan_inds] = np.nan\n\n    return out\n\ndef dff(f,\n        trial_starts,\n        teleports,\n        f_neu=None,\n        neuropil_method=None,\n        baseline_method='maximin',\n        subtract_baseline=True,\n        neu_coef=0.7,\n        tau=0.7,\n        frame_rate=15,\n        n_planes=1,\n        deconvolve=False,\n        keep_teleports=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate dFF for 1 channel\n\n    :param f: ROI fluorescence timeseries\n    :param trial_starts:\n    :param teleports:\n    :param f_neu: neuropil fluorescence timeseries\n    :param baseline_method:\n    :param subtract_baseline: True gives ∆F/F, False just normalizes F to the basline (divides only)\n    :param neu_coef:\n    :param deconvolve: whether to deconvolve the dff trace into \"events\" (psuedo spikes)\n    :return: dff and spks if deconvolve is True, otherwise just dff\n\n    `trial_starts` and `teleports` must be pandas Series: the keep_teleports branch calls\n    `.to_list()` on `teleports`, which a numpy array does not have. That is their code, unchanged.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff **\n    Keeps only the branches this dataset can reach (single-channel)\n    Dropped: bleedthrough_ts, neu_bleedthrough_ts, regress_ts, scrub_ts, neuropil_method='regress',\n    baseline_method='maxsmooth', subtract_baseline=False. \n    \"\"\"\n\n    print('baseline method =', baseline_method)\n\n    # Check frame rate if deconvolving\n    if deconvolve:\n        if (frame_rate/n_planes) > 16:\n            warnings.warn(\n                f\"Using frame rate per plane: {(frame_rate/n_planes)} -- is this correct?\")\n\n    f_ = np.zeros(f.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if f_neu is not None:\n        f_neu_ = np.zeros(f_neu.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if keep_teleports:\n        # if keeping the teleports, keep all the fluorescence from the start of imaging\n        # EXCEPT the teleport sample itself, as this position values might be interpolated between\n        # the end of the track and -50 (where the teleport jitter starts)\n        print(\"keeping teleports\")\n        start_inds = np.append(\n            trial_starts[0],\n            np.array(teleports[:-1] + 2)\n        ).tolist()\n        # ^ add 2 so with start-1 we will start 1 sample after the teleport\n        stop_inds = teleports.to_list()\n        # we will subtract 1 off this index below\n    else:\n        # if not keeping the teleports, keep only the fluorescence on each trial\n        start_inds = trial_starts.tolist()\n        stop_inds = teleports.tolist()\n\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        f_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n\n        if f_neu is not None:\n            f_neu_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f_neu[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n\n    # once bleedthrough is subtracted, do neuropil correction\n\n    if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n        f_ -= neu_coef * f_neu_\n\n    print('No minimum subtraction')\n\n    # Caluclate baseline for chan 1\n    flow = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n    spks = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n\n    # get baseline within each trial\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n\n        # if we subtracted neuropil, add back in the neuropil mean in each trial\n        # such that dff values will be close to true ∆F/F, and we don't divide by small numbers\n        if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n            f_[:, start-1:stop-1] = f_[:, start-1:stop-1] + neu_coef * np.nanmean(\n                f_neu_[:, start-1:stop-1], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n\n        if baseline_method == 'maximin':\n            # cut out ITIs and smooth signal\n            flow[:, start - 1:stop -\n                 1] = nansmooth(f_[:, start-1:stop-1], [0, 15])\n\n            # minimum filter, taking min val over 20 sec\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1) # was sp.ndimage.filters.minimum_filter1d\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1)  # max filter with same window (dilation), was sp.ndimage.filters.maximum_filter1d\n\n    # to get deltaF/F: subtract baseline from initial signal, divide by abs(baseline)\n    # baseline can some times end up as negative due to regression\n    dff = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n    if subtract_baseline:\n        dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]\n                           ) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n\n    print('dff:', dff.shape, 'min ', np.nanmin(dff), 'max ', np.nanmax(dff))\n\n    # Smooth the deltaF/F transients by 2 time bins\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        dff[:, start - 1:stop -\n            1] = nansmooth_1d(dff[:, start - 1:stop - 1], 2, axis=1)\n        # After smoothing: Get \"spikes\" with calcium kernel deconvolution from suite2p\n        if deconvolve:\n            spks[:, start-1:stop-1] = dcnv.oasis(\n                np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, start-1:stop-1], dtype=np.float32),\n                2000, tau, (frame_rate/n_planes)) # this is float64 in the original code\n\n    if deconvolve:\n        return dff, spks\n    else:\n        return dff\n\n\ndef is_putative_interneuron(dff, speed, r_thresh=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find putative interneurons based on correlation of dF/F with speed\n    (under the hypothesis that fast-spiking interneurons in the pyramidal\n    layer are often highly correlated with running speed).\n\n    :param dff: (ncells, T) dF/F timeseries, NaN outside the analysed windows\n    :param speed: (T,) running speed in cm/s\n    :param r_thresh: threshold for speed correlation r; dayData sets int_thresh = 0.5, which is\n        the value the Methods quote (\"a Pearson correlation of >0.5\"). Note that the threshold\n        defaulted to 0.3 in the source function; the analyses always pass 0.5.\n    :return: is_int (array) with a Boolean for each ROI, where putative interneuron is True.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/spatial.py::is_putative_interneuron **\n    Change: takes the dF/F and speed arrays as arguments instead of reading them off a TwoPUtils \n    `sess` object, which this conversion does not build.\n    \"\"\"\n    speed_corr = np.zeros((dff.shape[0],))\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n\n    for c in range(dff.shape[0]):\n        speed_corr[c] = np.corrcoef(\n            dff[c, nanmask], speed[nanmask])[0, 1]\n\n    is_int = speed_corr > r_thresh\n\n    return is_int\n\n# end copy-paste\n# =================================================================================================\n\n\ndef get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n    # Overview\n- **Dataset**: A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\n- **Date started**: 2026-03-11\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `create_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | LOADING | Creates a `TwoPUtils.sess.Session` object from imaging + VR files, then appends aligned session data. |\n| `append_session_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | Aligns VR to imaging, loads suite2p outputs, adds timeseries (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`) and position-binned trial matrices. |\n| `dff` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | Computes trial-restricted dF/F with neuropil subtraction, `maximin` baseline, smoothing, and optional OASIS deconvolution to `events`. |\n| `multi_anim_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/utilities.py` | LOADING | Loads saved `sess` pickles across animals for an experiment day and attaches dF/F, events, place-cell outputs, reward labels, and trial subsets. |\n| `quick_load_multi_anim_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/utilities.py` | LOADING | Loads already processed multi-animal pickle files from the shared preprocessed data location. |\n| `get_trial_types` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Computes per-trial reward outcome and environment morph from aligned VR data between `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. |\n| `get_reward_zones` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Maps scene names to reward-zone coordinates and labels (`A`, `B`, `C`) for each trial, including switch sessions. |\n| `define_trial_subsets` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Splits trials into trial sets based on reward-zone switches or halves for single-zone sessions. |\n| `get_timeseries_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py` | PROCESSING | Builds continuously sampled decoder-ready behavior and neural arrays from `sess.timeseries['events']`, masking low-speed and invalid samples. |\n| `CircularRegression` | `code/src/reward_relative/decode.py` | PROCESSING | Paper decoder model for circular variables; confirms the reference decoding was done on neural activity against position-like targets. |\n\n### Notes\n- The reference repo is for **2-photon calcium imaging**, not electrophysiology. There is no spike sorting or ephys quality filtering in this code path.\n- `sess` is the base aligned session object. The README explicitly states raw `sess` objects **do not contain dF/F**; dF/F is computed later.\n- `make_session_pkl.md` shows the first saved object is `sess`, created from raw suite2p + VR files after manual suite2p curation.\n- Cell curation is manual in the suite2p GUI; `iscell.npy` is the curated cell mask used when loading suite2p outputs.\n- `multi_anim_sess` is the main post-processing stage used by the paper:\n  - loads saved `sess` pickle for each animal/day,\n  - computes dF/F,\n  - optionally deconvolves dF/F into `events`,\n  - adds position-binned trial matrices,\n  - computes place-cell statistics and trial subset metadata.\n- For single-channel data, `multi_anim_sess(..., calc_spks=True)` calls `preprocessing.dff(...)` with:\n  - `neuropil_method='subtract'`\n  - `baseline_method='maximin'`\n  - `subtract_baseline=True`\n  - `neu_coef=0.7`\n  - `deconvolve=True`\n  - optional `keep_teleports` per animal\n- `dff` restricts valid fluorescence to trial spans using `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. Baseline is computed **within each trial** after smoothing, min-filtering, and max-filtering. The resulting dF/F is smoothed again, then OASIS is applied to get `events`.\n- `append_session_data` adds behavioral timeseries aligned to imaging frames. The pipeline is therefore based on **frame-aligned** neural and behavior streams.\n- `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` is the clearest reference for paper-style decoder inputs:\n  - neural data are `sess.timeseries['events']`\n  - trial boundaries use `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`\n  - reward-relative position is computed per sample from the reward-zone start for that trial\n  - low-speed samples are masked out with `use_speed_thr=2`\n  - lick sensor artifacts are suppressed when cumulative licks are implausibly high\n  - samples with NaNs in neural data or masked speed/lick channels are dropped\n- Reward-zone labels come from `behavior.get_reward_zones`:\n  - `A` corresponds to zone `X` = `[80, 130]`\n  - `B` corresponds to zone `Y` = `[200, 250]`\n  - `C` corresponds to zone `Z` = `[320, 370]`\n  - environment names remain `Env1`, `Env2`, `Env3`\n- The Fig. 3 decoder notebook uses:\n  - experiment `MetaLearn`\n  - experiment days `[3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14]`\n  - `ts_key='dff'` when loading saved `multiDayData`, but continuous neural decoder inputs are assembled from `sess.timeseries['events']` via `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`\n  - occupancy matching by reward-relative position bin for some paper analyses\n- Conclusion for conversion:\n  - **Yes, dF/F must be computed** to match the reference processing.\n  - The decoder-relevant neural stream is most likely the **deconvolved `events`**, not raw fluorescence.\n  - Trial alignment should use `trial_start_inds` as the start and `teleport_inds` as the end of each trial.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `data/` contains one NWB file per subject-session plus `dandiset.yaml`.\n- Subject directories present:\n  - `sub-m3`, `sub-m4`, `sub-m7`, `sub-m11`, `sub-m12`, `sub-m13`, `sub-m14`, `sub-m15`, `sub-m17`, `sub-m18`, `sub-m19`\n- File pattern:\n  - `data/sub-<mouse>/sub-<mouse>_ses-<NN>_behavior+ophys.nwb`\n- Total NWB files: `152`\n- Sessions per subject from file inventory:\n  - `sub-m11`: 12 sessions (`ses-03` to `ses-14`)\n  - all other listed subjects: 14 sessions each\n- Each NWB contains:\n  - `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries`\n  - `processing/ophys/Fluorescence`\n  - `processing/ophys/Neuropil`\n  - `processing/ophys/Deconvolved`\n  - `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation`\n- Behavioral time series present in every sampled file:\n  - `Reward`\n  - `autoreward`\n  - `environment`\n  - `lick`\n  - `position`\n  - `reward_zone`\n  - `scanning`\n  - `speed`\n  - `teleport`\n  - `trial number`\n  - `trial_start`\n- Ophys organization in sample NWB:\n  - `Deconvolved/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n  - `Fluorescence/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n  - `Neuropil/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n  - `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation` columns:\n    - `pixel_mask`\n    - `iscell`\n    - `planeIdx`\n- `iscell` has shape `(n_rois, 2)` and matches suite2p format:\n  - column 0: binary cell flag (`0` or `1`)\n  - column 1: suite2p confidence/probability\n- Imaging metadata from sample file:\n  - indicator: `GCaMP7f`\n  - location: `hippocampus, CA1`\n- Neural sampling rates present across the dataset:\n  - `15.5078125 Hz` in 124 sessions\n  - `31.015625 Hz` in 28 sessions\n- Frame-count alignment:\n  - most sessions have equal behavior/neural frame counts\n  - 10 sessions are off by exactly 1 sample (`behavior = neural - 1`), so edge handling will be required\n- Behavioral coding observed directly from NWB values:\n  - `environment`: values `-1, 0, 1` (`-1` appears outside valid trial/imaging periods)\n  - `trial number`: values `-1` plus contiguous nonnegative trial ids\n  - `trial_start`, `teleport`, `lick`, `scanning`: binary-like 0/1 signals with some `-1` outside valid periods\n  - `Reward`: sparse event series with separate timestamps (not frame-length)\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | `118,493` curated cells using `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` on the ROI indices referenced by `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`; `260,091` total ROIs in the neural response matrices |\n| Neurons / session | curated mean `779.56` (min `155`, max `1780`); raw ROI mean `1711.12` (min `315`, max `3934`) |\n| Subjects | `11` |\n| Sessions / subject | mostly `14`; `sub-m11` has `12` |\n| Trials (total) | `12,216` from `sum(trial_start > 0)` across sessions |\n| Trials / session | mean `80.37` (min `41`, max `100`) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | Not directly reported | Methods report per-session counts rather than a total. |\n| Neurons / session | `155–2172` putative pyramidal neurons/session | “155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session” |\n| Subjects | `11` switch-task mice in main dataset; `3` fixed-condition mice in separate cohort | “switch task (n = 11 mice)” and “fixed-condition cohort (n = 3 mice)” |\n| Sessions / subject | `14` imaging days for task; `m11` starts imaging on day 3 | “total of 14 days” and “for m11, imaging started on day 3” |\n| Trials (total) | `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (for licking QC reference) | “81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice” |\n| Trials / session | target `80–100`; mean `80.5 ± 7.4` across all imaging days | “We targeted 80–100 trials per session… mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials” |\n| Neural data time bin | imaging sampled at `~15.5 Hz` per plane | “sampled at ~15.5 Hz” / “~15.5 Hz per plane” |\n| Behavior data time bin | behavior aligned to imaging frame rate `~15.5 Hz` | “All behavioral and neural time series were sampled at ~15.5 Hz” |\n| Reward rate | `~85% rewarded`, `~15% omitted` | “Reward was randomly omitted on approximately 15% of trials” |\n| Reward switch timing | switch after `30` trials | “Each switch occurred after 30 trials” |\n| Reward zones | A=`80–130 cm`, B=`200–250 cm`, C=`320–370 cm` | “zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm” |\n| Track length | `450 cm` | “450 cm linear track” |\n| Decoder analysis set | `77 sessions`, `11 mice`, `7` switch days | “n = 77 sessions, 11 mice, seven switch days” |\n| Decoder general result | before-switch: RR/TR/non-RR above shuffle; after-switch: only RR above shuffle | “when we tested on trials after the reward switch, only the RR population decoded… better than shuffle” |\n| Decoder spatial extent | z-scored decode `>2` from `−104.5 ± 20.1 cm` to `+152.7 ± 22.9 cm` relative to reward | “range of z-scored decode >2: −104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm” |\n| GLM performance reference | FDE all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` | “On average, the model’s FDE…” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Task structure:\n  - hidden 50 cm reward zone on a 450 cm linear VR track\n  - two main environments (`ENV 1`, `ENV 2`) in the switch task\n  - reward locations A/B/C are equidistant and only one is active at a time\n  - reward switches occur after 30 trials on switch days\n  - first 10 trials of a new condition can receive automatic reward if the mouse fails to lick in the new zone\n  - teleport period follows each lap: gray jitter period plus 50 cm tunnel before the next lap\n- Imaging / alignment:\n  - 2-photon CA1 calcium imaging with GCaMP7f\n  - most sessions sampled at ~15.5 Hz\n  - m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes interleaved at ~31 Hz, yielding ~15.5 Hz per plane\n  - imaging sometimes stopped during teleport periods to minimize photodamage; some animals/days were imaged through teleports\n- Neural preprocessing:\n  - Suite2P used for motion correction and ROI detection\n  - dF/F baseline computed independently within each trial using a `maximin` procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n  - dF/F = `(F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n  - dF/F smoothed with a two-sample Gaussian\n  - deconvolved activity extracted with OASIS and used for decoder / SI analyses\n- Behavioral / neural analysis conventions:\n  - all spatial analyses exclude neural activity when speed `< 2 cm/s`\n  - spatial binning is 45 bins of 10 cm each along the 450 cm track\n  - licking is converted to binary counts and smoothed / binned for downstream analyses\n- Decoder-specific paper processing:\n  - decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity\n  - tenfold cross-validation, 90% train / 10% test\n  - train/test either within pre-switch trials or train pre-switch and test post-switch\n  - occupancy is downsampled to match reward-relative position bins\n  - shuffled controls are generated by circularly shifting each cell’s timeseries\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs:\n  - with multiple somata or dendrites\n  - lacking visually obvious transients\n  - suspected of calcium-indicator overexpression\n  - with high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n- Additional putative interneurons were excluded if Pearson correlation between dF/F and running speed exceeded `0.5`\n- Reported additional exclusion from this speed-correlation rule: `0.42 ± 0.85%` of cells across mice/days\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Reward omission trials occur randomly on ~15% of trials and are retained as a task condition\n- Licking analysis removes trials with erroneous lick detection when >30% of imaging frames in the trial have cumulative lick count `> 2`\n- Reported lick-artifact removal: `81 / 12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (~0.65%)\n- Some analyses require at least three omission trials within a trial set\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Reward-relative position from deconvolved activity | Paper reports decode score above shuffle, not classification accuracy; after-switch generalization is significant only for RR cells |\n| Single-neuron deconvolved activity from task + movement variables (GLM) | FDE: all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Cohort size | Code primarily analyzes the `MetaLearn` switch cohort by day, especially the seven switch days | NWB archive contains 11 mice / 152 subject-session files | Paper reports 11 switch-task mice plus a separate 3-mouse fixed-condition cohort | Treat the shared NWB archive as the 11-mouse switch cohort only. Fixed-condition mice are not present in `data/`. |\n| Session count | Code expects one saved `sess` per imaging day; m11 has special handling in metadata | 152 NWB files = `11 * 14 - 2` | Paper states 14 task days, with imaging for m11 starting on day 3 | Counts are consistent once the m11 day 1-2 exception is applied. |\n| Neural stream to decode | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` uses `sess.timeseries['events']` after dF/F + OASIS | NWB stores `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved`; no explicit dF/F timeseries | Paper decoder uses deconvolved calcium activity derived after per-trial dF/F | Use NWB `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` as the paper-equivalent neural signal. Raw `Fluorescence`/`Neuropil` remain available for sanity checks. |\n| Cell filtering source | Code assumes suite2p curation has already been applied to the loaded `sess` data | NWB includes `iscell` in `PlaneSegmentation`, but segmentation tables for multipane mice contain ROIs not present in `Deconvolved/plane0/data` | Paper counts are post-curation putative pyramidal neurons | Filter cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` only on ROI indices listed in `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`. This avoids overcounting plane-1 ROIs that are not in the response matrix. |\n| Neurons/session | Code/paper operate on curated cells after ROI QC | Initial naive data count from full segmentation exceeded paper range; corrected ROI-referenced curated count is `155–1780` per session | Paper reports `155–2172` neurons/session | After using the ROI region correctly, the data are consistent with the paper’s reported range. |\n| Multipane sessions | Code notes m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes and pooled for most analyses | NWB sessions for `sub-m17` and `sub-m18` have response rate `31.015625 Hz`, `planeIdx` includes `0/1`, but `Deconvolved/plane0/rois` selects only plane 0 cells | Paper states two-plane acquisition at ~31 Hz interleaved, ~15.5 Hz per plane | The archive exposes only the plane-0 response series for these files. Conversion will use the provided response matrix and document this archive-level limitation. |\n| Trial boundary representation | Code uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` arrays in `sess` | NWB provides framewise binary `trial_start` and `teleport` time series plus `trial number` | Paper defines laps/trials with a teleport period after each lap | Reconstruct trial start/end indices from `trial_start` and `teleport` signals in NWB. |\n| Timebase | Code and methods emphasize ~15.5 Hz per plane | NWB contains both `15.5078125 Hz` and `31.015625 Hz` sessions | Paper says most sessions ~15.5 Hz and multipane mice interleaved at ~31 Hz for ~15.5 Hz/plane | Use a common output bin size during conversion so all sessions share the same decoder timebase. |\n| Trial totals | Data inventory gives `12,216` starts / `12,217` unique trial numbers | NWB trial counts are slightly lower than one paper count | Paper cites `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice in the lick-QC section and `80.5 ± 7.4` trials/session overall | Accept NWB-exported trial identities as ground truth for conversion, while using the paper’s mean trials/session as the stronger consistency check. The remaining ~1.3% mismatch is likely a paper-vs-export counting convention difference and will be tracked in later sanity checks. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Crop to valid frame range, select ROI indices from `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`, keep curated cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5`, optionally exclude speed-correlated interneuron-like cells, transpose to `(neurons, time)`, split by trials, and rebin `31.015625 Hz` sessions to the common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size | `preprocessing.dff`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess`, `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Neural decoder signal should match paper deconvolved activity (`events`) as closely as possible. |\n| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/rois` + `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` | `neural` / `brain_region_idx` | Use ROI region referenced by response matrix, then `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` to keep curated cells only | suite2p curation assumption in `make_session_pkl.md`; `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Critical for multipane sessions; do not use the full segmentation table. |\n| `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/location` | `brain_regions` | Normalize `hippocampus, CA1` to `CA1` | N/A | All sessions are CA1 in this archive. |\n| Reconstructed trial-relative elapsed time | `input[0]` | Seconds since trial start for each bin | trial handling implicit in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Align all trials to the first frame of the trial. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data` | `input[1]` | Per trial, take modal valid environment value (`0` or `1`) and repeat across bins | session/task logic in `behavior.get_trial_types` / paper task description | Map `0 -> ENV1`, `1 -> ENV2`. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/trial number/data` | `input[2]` | Per trial, use within-session trial index and repeat across bins | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` creates 0-indexed `trial_ids` | Keep native 0-indexing to match code conventions. |\n| Trial reward outcome shifted by one trial | `input[3]` | Previous trial rewarded (`1`) vs omitted (`0`), repeated across current-trial bins; first trial gets `0` | paper reward omission schedule; reward outcome from trial parsing | User-requested decoder input, not a paper decoder variable. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` + inferred reward-zone bounds | `output[0]` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, `0` inside zone, positive after zone; discretize to 7 bins per task spec | reward-zone logic from `behavior.get_reward_zones`; reward-relative computations in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | For zone `[start, end]`: `pos < start -> pos-start`; `start <= pos <= end -> 0`; `pos > end -> pos-end`. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` | `output[1]` | Discretize absolute position on the 450 cm corridor into 5 equal bins | paper: 45 spatial bins over 450 cm | Decoder spec requires 5 bins, not the paper’s 45 bins. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed/data` | `output[2]` | Discretize speed into bins: `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40 cm/s` | paper and code use speed threshold 2 cm/s | Keep all bins for output even though paper excludes `<2 cm/s` in some analyses. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick/data` | `output[3]` | Convert to binary per bin (`lick > 0`) after lick-artifact handling | lick cleanup in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`; methods licking QC | For rebinned 31 Hz sessions, use logical OR within each 15.5 Hz bin. |\n| Inferred active reward zone per trial | `output[4]` | Map trial reward location to categorical `{A:0, B:1, C:2}` and repeat across trial bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones`; paper task design | Infer from reward-zone-active positions / reward events and fill omission trials within stable blocks. |\n| Reward events assigned to trials | `output[5]` | Trial outcome categorical `{omitted:0, rewarded:1}` repeated across trial bins | `behavior.get_trial_types` | Rewarded if any `Reward` event timestamp falls within the trial. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use all 152 NWB sessions from the 11 switch-task mice**: The shared archive corresponds to the main switch cohort, not the 3 fixed-condition mice.\n1. **Use NWB `Deconvolved` as the neural signal**: The paper’s decoder and SI analyses operate on deconvolved activity after dF/F, and this stream is already provided in the archive.\n1. **Filter cells with ROI-region-aware `iscell` selection**: Only ROI ids referenced by the response matrix are valid candidates for inclusion.\n1. **Plan to exclude putative interneuron-like cells**: The paper excludes cells with dF/F-speed correlation `> 0.5`, so the conversion script should implement this if feasible from the NWB fluorescence streams and reference `dff` logic.\n1. **Reconstruct trials from `trial_start` and `teleport`**: This matches the reference trial definition more closely than relying only on trial-number changes.\n1. **Standardize all sessions to a common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size**: This is the dominant dataset rate and the paper’s effective per-plane sampling rate.\n1. **Use time-varying outputs for all decoder targets**: Even per-trial outputs (`reward zone`, `reward outcome`) will be repeated across bins for consistent `(n_output, n_timepoints)` arrays.\n1. **Infer reward-zone location from rewarded trials and fill omission trials within stable blocks**: Missing zone labels in the NWB export are concentrated on omission trials; the task structure constrains the active zone to one contiguous block per condition.\n1. **Use omission fraction and switch timing as sanity anchors**: Raw data show `rewarded = 84.65%`, `omission = 15.35%`, matching the paper’s ~15% omission rate, and switch sessions should change condition near trial 30.\n1. **Keep within-session trial number 0-indexed**: This matches raw NWB values and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural spot-check: for one single-plane session and one 31 Hz session, compare a raw NWB trial slice against converted `neural` with `np.allclose()` after ROI filtering and rebinning.\n- [ ] Input spot-check: verify `time_from_trial_start`, `environment`, `trial number`, and `previous outcome` on 3 hand-checked trials loaded directly from NWB.\n- [ ] Output spot-check: verify distance-to-zone bin, absolute-position bin, speed bin, and lick bin on specific raw frames loaded directly from NWB.\n- [ ] Trial parsing check: confirm converted trial counts match reconstructed `trial_start` / `teleport` boundaries.\n- [ ] Cohort statistics check: converted subjects/sessions/trials/neurons must match the NWB inventory and remain within paper-reported ranges.\n- [ ] Outcome distribution check: rewarded/omission fraction in converted data should remain close to the raw-data fraction (`84.65% / 15.35%`).\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n[Implementation notes]\n- Created `convert_data.py` with:\n  - NWB file discovery and sample/full session selection\n  - ROI-region-aware `iscell` filtering\n  - trial reconstruction from `trial_start` / `teleport`\n  - trial-wise reward outcome and reward-zone inference\n  - 31 Hz to 15.5078125 Hz rebinning\n  - trial-aligned neural/input/output construction\n  - metadata export and optional processing plots\n- First execution on `--sample --show-processing` completed without runtime errors.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Full-session NWB reads currently materialize the session response matrix in memory once per session.\n- Processing plots intentionally add overhead and should stay disabled for full conversion.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Used direct `h5py` reads instead of heavier NWB object materialization.\n- Restricted neural loading to ROI indices actually referenced by the response matrix and then to curated `iscell` ROIs.\n- Used vectorized trial construction and simple factor-2 rebinning for 31 Hz sessions.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: common rate; example trial and trial-wise reward-zone summary look internally consistent.\n- No obvious temporal misalignment or discretization artifacts seen in either sample plot.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n| Direct `h5py` loading instead of full NWB object graph | Keeps per-session conversion near ~1.3 s in the sample run |\n| ROI-region-aware cell filtering before downstream work | Avoids processing unused segmentation-table ROIs |\n| Simple factor-2 rebin for 31 Hz sessions | Keeps multipane-session harmonization inexpensive |\n\n| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n| Conversion core (observed sample sessions) | `1.22-1.35 s/session` | `~3.5 min` for 152 sessions without processing plots |\n# Converted Switch-Task Dataset\n\nThis directory contains a decoder-ready conversion of the Sosa et al. hippocampal imaging dataset from the paper *A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward*.\n\n## Dataset Summary\n- Subjects: `11` mice\n- Sessions: `152`\n- Trials retained after conversion: `12,147`\n- Curated neurons: `118,493`\n- Brain region: `CA1`\n- Common time bin: `64.483 ms` (`15.5078125 Hz`)\n- Temporal alignment: trial start\n\nThe converted pickle is [`converted_data.pkl`](/app/converted_data.pkl). A smaller test subset is [`sample_data.pkl`](/app/sample_data.pkl).\n\n## Files\n- [`convert_data.py`](/app/convert_data.py): conversion script\n- [`converted_data.pkl`](/app/converted_data.pkl): full converted dataset\n- [`sample_data.pkl`](/app/sample_data.pkl): 2-session sample dataset\n- [`CONVERSION_NOTES.md`](/app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md): detailed step-by-step audit trail\n\n## Conversion Choices\n- Neural signal: `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data`\n- Cell curation: ROI ids from `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`, then `iscell[:,0] > 0.5`\n- Trial parsing: reconstructed from `trial_start` and `teleport`\n- Multipane sessions (`m17`, `m18`): rebinned from `31.015625 Hz` to the common `15.5078125 Hz`\n- Lick-artifact filtering: drop trials where more than `35%` of frames have `lick > 2`, matching the reference code path in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`\n\n## Output Structure\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    \"neural\": [session][trial] -> float32 array (n_neurons, n_timepoints),\n    \"input\": [session][trial] -> float32 array (4, n_timepoints),\n    \"output\": [session][trial] -> int64 array (6, n_timepoints),\n    \"subjects\": list[str],\n    \"subject_idx\": int array (n_sessions,),\n    \"brain_regions\": list[str],\n    \"brain_region_idx\": [session] -> int array (n_neurons,),\n    \"input_names\": list[str],\n    \"output_names\": list[str],\n    \"output_values\": list[list[str]],\n    \"metadata\": dict,\n}\n```\n\nInputs:\n1. `time_from_trial_start_s`\n2. `environment`\n3. `trial_number`\n4. `previous_trial_rewarded`\n\nOutputs:\n1. `distance_to_reward_zone`\n2. `absolute_position`\n3. `speed`\n4. `lick`\n5. `reward_zone_location`\n6. `reward_outcome`\n\n## Usage\n\nConvert the full
